  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. When the file guard flags a span, fix it and say nothing. This skill should be used when the user is looking for functionality that might exist as an installable skill.\n- implementing-react-i18n: Use when adding, fixing, or reviewing React internationalization, language selectors, locale resources, translated UI copy, or bugs where changing language does not update user-facing strings\n- improve: Survey any codebase as a senior advisor and produce prioritized, self-contained implementation plans for OTHER models/agents to execute. Strictly read-only on source code — never implements, fixes, or refactors anything itself. Use when asked to audit a codebase, find improvement opportunities (bugs, security, performance, test coverage, tech debt, migrations, DX), suggest features or where to take the project next (roadmap, product direction), or generate handoff plans for another agent to implement.\n- instrumenting-control-planes: Use when building, planning, reviewing, or debugging apps with backend decisions, agent workflows, long-running jobs, SSE/WebSocket streams, recovery paths, deletes, retries, or user-visible failures\n- learning-from-bugs: Use when a meaningful bug, regression, architecture flaw, storage issue, memory problem, persistence failure, control-plane failure, or system-level failure has been diagnosed or fixed\n- optimizing-react-next-apps: Use when a React or Next.js app feels slow, cluttered, overbundled, slow to compile, slow to load, ineffective at SSR, hydration-heavy, or likely burdened by unnecessary imports or UI dependencies\n- receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- second-opinion: Use when writing or hardening substantial specs, implementation plans, architecture plans, design plans, or unusually hard creative decisions before execution\n- subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- using-ultrapowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before acting\n- verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- writing-plans: Use when you have an approved spec or clear requirements for a multi-step task, before implementation\n- writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- sloptrim:sloptrim: Use when the user wants to humanize text, trim slop, de-AI or de-slop writing, remove AI tells, fix robotic or ChatGPT-sounding prose, or make writing sound human and natural. Also run before delivering a CV, cover letter, email, report, or essay to be sent. Mode-aware, so it never fabricates voice on factual content.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. - When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.

Testing:
- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.

Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.

Frontend i18n:
- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.

React Best Practices:
- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
- NEVER send data around in transactions.
- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.

);

19	export async function syncPlanItems(planId: string, items: ParsedPlanItem[]) {

          try {

            // ephemeral scratch files that can be gone by the time a run is
            // resumed, and readSupervisorFile's bare statSync would otherwise

            // fails the resume. Surface the read failure back to the

1	import fs from "fs";
2	import path from "path";
3	import { randomUUID } from "crypto";
4	import { getAppDataPath } from "@/server/app-root";
5	
6	interface AttachmentInput {
7	  kind?: string;
8	  name?: string;
9	  path?: string;
10	  storagePath?: string;
11	  mimeType?: string;
12	  size?: number;
13	}
14	
15	function quoteBlock(text: string) {
16	  return text
17	    .split(/\r?\n/)
18	    .map((line) => `> ${line}`)
19	    .join("\n");
20	}
21	
22	function formatAttachments(attachments: AttachmentInput[]) {
23	  if (attachments.length === 0) return "";
24	
25	  const lines = attachments.map((attachment) => {
26	    const parts = [
27	      attachment.kind?.trim(),
28	      attachment.name?.trim(),
29	      attachment.mimeType?.trim(),
30	      typeof attachment.size === "number" ? `${attachment.size} bytes` : undefined,
31	      attachment.storagePath?.trim() || attachment.path?.trim(),
32	    ].filter(Boolean);
33	
34	    return parts.length > 0 ? `- ${parts.join(" | ")}` : "- attachment";
35	  });
36	
37	  return `\nAttachments:\n\n${lines.join("\n")}\n`;
38	}
39	
40	export function buildAdHocPlanMarkdown(command: string, attachments: AttachmentInput[] = []) {
41	  return `# Ad Hoc Request
42	
43	Original command:
44	
45	${quoteBlock(command)}
46	${formatAttachments(attachments)}
47	
48	## Supervisor Notes
49	
50	This file stores the original request and any attached context for the supervisor.
51	`;
52	}
53	
54	export function createAdHocPlan(command: string, attachments: AttachmentInput[] = []) {
55	  const adHocDir = getAppDataPath("vibes", "ad-hoc");
56	  fs.mkdirSync(adHocDir, { recursive: true });
57	
58	  const filename = `${new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, "-")}-${randomUUID()}.md`;
59	  const relativePath = path.join("vibes", "ad-hoc", filename);
60	  fs.writeFileSync(getAppDataPath(relativePath), buildAdHocPlanMarkdown(command, attachments), "utf-8");
61	  return relativePath;
62	}
63	
64	export function rewriteAdHocPlan(relativePath: string, command: string, attachments: AttachmentInput[] = []) {
65	  fs.writeFileSync(getAppDataPath(relativePath), buildAdHocPlanMarkdown(command, attachments), "utf-8");
66	}

262	export function normalizeAcpGoalPlanUpdate(
263	  value: unknown,
264	  context: { goalId: string; revision: number },
265	): AcpGoalPlanResult {
266	  const envelopeFailure = inspectEnvelope(value);
267	  if (envelopeFailure) return { ok: false, reason: envelopeFailure };
268	  if (!isGoalRecord(value) || typeof value.sessionUpdate !== "string") return { ok: false, reason: "malformed" };
269	
270	  if (value.sessionUpdate === "plan") {
271	    return normalizePlanItems(value.entries, context);
272	  }
273	  if (value.sessionUpdate === "plan_removed") {
274	    const providerPlanId = cleanString(value.id);
275	    if (!providerPlanId) return { ok: false, reason: "malformed" };
276	    return { ok: true, value: { providerPlanId, plan: [], planSource: { kind: "none" }, removed: true } };
277	  }
278	  if (value.sessionUpdate !== "plan_update" || !isGoalRecord(value.plan)) {
279	    return { ok: false, reason: "malformed" };
280	  }
281	  const providerPlanId = cleanString(value.plan.id);
282	  if (!providerPlanId) return { ok: false, reason: "malformed" };
283	  if (value.plan.type === "items") {
284	    const normalized = normalizePlanItems(value.plan.entries, context);
285	    return normalized.ok
286	      ? { ok: true, value: { ...normalized.value, providerPlanId } }
287	      : normalized;
288	  }
289	  if (value.plan.type === "markdown") {
290	    if (typeof value.plan.content !== "string" || value.plan.content.length > GOAL_PLAN_MARKDOWN_MAX_LENGTH) {
291	      return { ok: false, reason: "too_large" };
292	    }
293	    return {
294	      ok: true,
295	      value: { providerPlanId, plan: [], planSource: { kind: "markdown", markdown: value.plan.content }, removed: false },
296	    };
297	  }
298	  if (value.plan.type === "file") {
299	    const uri = safePlanUri(value.plan.uri);
300	    if (!uri) return { ok: false, reason: "unsafe_uri" };
301	    return { ok: true, value: { providerPlanId, plan: [], planSource: { kind: "uri", uri }, removed: false } };
302	  }
303	  return { ok: false, reason: "malformed" };
304	}

306	export function normalizeGoalFallbackCapabilities(availableCommands: readonly string[]) {
307	  const commands = new Set(availableCommands.map((command) => command.trim().replace(/^\//, "").toLowerCase()));
308	  const hasGoal = commands.has("goal");

export interface PlannerHandoff {
  specPath: string | null;
  planPath: string | null;
  ready: boolean;
  summary: string | null;
}

export interface PlannerArtifactCandidate {
  path: string;
  kind: "spec" | "plan" | "unknown";
  source: "handoff" | "output_text" | "inferred";
  confidence: number;
  evidence: string;
  exists: boolean;
  readiness?: PlanReadinessAssessment | null;
  readinessRecord?: PlanReadinessRecord | null;
}

export interface PlannerArtifacts {
  specPath: string | null;
  planPath: string | null;
  candidates: PlannerArtifactCandidate[];
}

function normalizeCandidatePath(cwd: string, candidate: string) {
  const trimmed = candidate.trim().replace(/^["'`]|["'`]$/g, "");
  if (!trimmed) {
    return null;
  }

  return path.isAbsolute(trimmed) ? path.normalize(trimmed) : path.resolve(cwd, trimmed);
}

function inferKind(candidatePath: string): PlannerArtifactCandidate["kind"] {
  const lower = candidatePath.toLowerCase();
  if (lower.includes("/specs/") || lower.includes("-design.md")) return "spec";
  if (lower.includes("/plans/") || lower.includes("plan")) return "plan";
  return "unknown";
}

function inferSiblingSpecPaths(planPath: string) {
  const normalizedPlanPath = path.normalize(planPath);
  const segments = normalizedPlanPath.split(path.sep);
  const plansIndex = segments.lastIndexOf("plans");
  if (plansIndex < 0) {
    return [];
  }

  const parsed = path.parse(normalizedPlanPath);
  const specsSegments = [...segments.slice(0, -1)];
  specsSegments[plansIndex] = "specs";
  const specsDir = specsSegments.join(path.sep) || path.sep;
  const baseNames = [
    parsed.name.replace(/-implementation$/, "-design"),
    parsed.name.replace(/-plan$/, "-design"),
    `${parsed.name}-design`,
    parsed.name,
  ];

  return Array.from(new Set(baseNames))
    .map((baseName) => path.join(specsDir, `${baseName}${parsed.ext || ".md"}`));
}

function extractMarkdownPaths(outputText: string) {
  const artifactReferencePattern = /\b(?:created|drafted|finalized|generated|handoff|prepared|saved|updated|wrote|spec_path|plan_path)\b/i;
  const markdownPathPattern = /(?:\/[\w./-]+|(?:docs|vibes)[\w./-]*|\.?\.?\/[\w./-]+)\.md\b/g;
  const matches: string[] = [];
  let artifactReferenceContextLines = 0;

  for (const line of outputText.split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const hasArtifactReference = artifactReferencePattern.test(line);
    if (hasArtifactReference) {
      artifactReferenceContextLines = 3;
    }

    if (!hasArtifactReference && artifactReferenceContextLines === 0) {
      continue;
    }

    matches.push(...line.matchAll(markdownPathPattern).map((match) => match[0]));

    if (!hasArtifactReference && artifactReferenceContextLines > 0) {
      artifactReferenceContextLines -= 1;
    }
  }

  return Array.from(new Set(matches));
}

  const match = matches.at(-1);
  if (!match) {

  let evidence = args.evidence;
  let exists = fs.existsSync(finalPath);

  if (exists && isInsideScratchRoot(finalPath)) {
    const target = copyIntoProjectStandardDir({ cwd: args.cwd, kind, sourcePath: finalPath });
    if (target) {

    if (relocated) {

export async function collectPlannerArtifacts(args: {
  cwd: string;
  outputText: string;
}): Promise<PlannerArtifacts> {
  const handoff = extractPlannerHandoffBlock(args.outputText);
  const candidates: PlannerArtifactCandidate[] = [];
  const seenPaths = new Set<string>();

  const handoffInputs = [
    ...(handoff?.specPath ? [{ rawPath: handoff.specPath, source: "handoff" as const, evidence: "spec_path in handoff block", confidence: 1 }] : []),
    ...(handoff?.planPath ? [{ rawPath: handoff.planPath, source: "handoff" as const, evidence: "plan_path in handoff block", confidence: 1 }] : []),
  ];
  const scratchFallbackInputs = extractScratchAbsolutePaths(args.outputText).map((rawPath) => ({
    rawPath,
    source: "output_text" as const,
    evidence: `agent scratch path mentioned in output: ${rawPath}`,
    confidence: 0.8,
  }));
  const candidateInputs = [
    ...handoffInputs,
    ...(handoffInputs.length > 0
      ? scratchFallbackInputs
      : [
        ...extractMarkdownPaths(args.outputText).map((rawPath) => ({
          rawPath,
          source: "output_text" as const,
          evidence: `mentioned in output: ${rawPath}`,
          confidence: 0.65,
        })),
        ...scratchFallbackInputs,
      ]),
  ];

  for (const input of candidateInputs) {
    const candidate = await buildCandidate({
      cwd: args.cwd,
      rawPath: input.rawPath,
      source: input.source,
      evidence: input.evidence,
      confidence: input.confidence,
    });
    if (!candidate || seenPaths.has(candidate.path)) {
      continue;
    }
    if (handoff?.ready && input.source === "handoff" && candidate.kind === "plan") {
      candidate.readiness = {
        ready: true,
        questions: [],
        gaps: [],
        structure: candidate.readiness?.structure ?? {
          itemCount: 0,
          hasAcceptanceCriteria: false,
          hasGoalSection: false,
          itemsMissingDetails: [],
          itemsMissingVerify: [],
          itemsWithEmptyVerify: [],
          itemsWithVagueTitle: [],
          itemsWithStubTitle: [],
        },
      };
    }

    seenPaths.add(candidate.path);
    candidates.push(candidate);
  }

  const explicitSpecCandidates = candidates.filter((candidate) => candidate.kind === "spec" && candidate.exists);
  if (explicitSpecCandidates.length === 0) {
    const detectedPlanCandidates = candidates.filter((candidate) => candidate.kind === "plan" && candidate.exists);
    for (const planCandidate of detectedPlanCandidates) {
      for (const inferredSpecPath of inferSiblingSpecPaths(planCandidate.path)) {
        if (seenPaths.has(inferredSpecPath) || !fs.existsSync(inferredSpecPath)) {
          continue;
        }

        const candidate = await buildCandidate({
          cwd: args.cwd,
          rawPath: inferredSpecPath,
          source: "inferred",
          evidence: `inferred from plan artifact: ${planCandidate.path}`,
          confidence: 0.75,
        });
        if (!candidate) {
          continue;
        }

        seenPaths.add(candidate.path);
        candidates.push(candidate);
      }
    }
  }

  const specCandidates = candidates.filter((candidate) => candidate.kind === "spec" && candidate.exists);
  const planCandidates = candidates.filter((candidate) => candidate.kind === "plan" && candidate.exists);

  const specPath = handoff?.specPath && specCandidates.length > 0
    ? specCandidates[0]?.path ?? null
    : specCandidates.length === 1
      ? specCandidates[0]?.path ?? null
      : null;

  const handoffPlanPath = handoff?.planPath
    ? normalizeCandidatePath(args.cwd, handoff.planPath)
    : null;
  const handoffPlanCandidate = planCandidates.find((candidate) =>
    candidate.source === "handoff" && (handoffPlanPath ? candidate.path === handoffPlanPath : true),
  ) ?? planCandidates.find((candidate) => candidate.source === "handoff");
  const scratchRelocatedPlanCandidate = planCandidates.find((candidate) =>
    candidate.evidence.includes("relocated from agent scratch dir"),
  );
  const planPath = handoffPlanCandidate
    ? handoffPlanCandidate.path
    : scratchRelocatedPlanCandidate
      ? scratchRelocatedPlanCandidate.path
      : planCandidates.length === 1
        ? planCandidates[0]?.path ?? null
        : null;

  return { specPath, planPath, candidates };

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:45:45.896Z

I'm working through the timestamps to figure out the actual timeline—the goal was set around 17:36 UTC, near the end of the run, while the run itself started around 15:26 UTC, giving roughly a 2 hour 9 minute window between the initial message and when the goal was set.

That goal.put call came from principal acp:05d9fad55def-worker-1, meaning the agent itself set its objective via an ACP extension, with an empty plan and no plan source. This suggests OmniHarness exposes a goal extension that the agent can call directly, but I want to trace whether the harness itself derives the goal from the user's /goal message and pushes it through this extension once the session is ready — the transition_source being "reconciliation" hints at that. I should search for where goal.put gets invoked to understand who's actually setting the objective.

30	async function resolveRunIdForGoalWorker(workerId: string): Promise<string | null> {
31	  const [{ eq }, { db }, { workers }] = await Promise.all([
32	    import("drizzle-orm"),
33	    import("@/server/db"),
34	    import("@/server/db/schema"),
35	  ]);
36	  const worker = await db.select({ runId: workers.runId }).from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();
37	  return worker?.runId ?? null;
38	}
39	
40	function goalMetadataEnvelope(update: unknown) {
41	  if (!isGoalRecord(update) || !isGoalRecord(update._meta) || !isGoalRecord(update._meta.goal)) return null;
42	  return update;
43	}
44	
45	export function isAcpGoalNotification(update: unknown) {
46	  if (goalMetadataEnvelope(update)) return true;
47	  if (!isGoalRecord(update)) return false;
48	  return update.sessionUpdate === "available_commands_update"
49	    || update.sessionUpdate === "plan"
50	    || update.sessionUpdate === "plan_update"
51	    || update.sessionUpdate === "plan_removed";
52	}
53	
54	async function emitGoalPayloadRejection(args: {
55	  runId: string;
56	  goalId: string;
57	  workerId: string;
58	  reason: string;
59	}) {
60	  const { emitNamedEvent } = await import("@/server/events/named-events");
61	  emitNamedEvent({ kind: "goal.payload_rejected", ...args });
62	  emitNamedEvent({
63	    kind: "error.surfaced",
64	    code: "goal.payload.invalid",
65	    message: `The agent sent an invalid goal update (${args.reason}).`,
66	    surface: "log",
67	    runId: args.runId,
68	    workerId: args.workerId,
69	  });
70	}
71	
72	export async function handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker(args: {
73	  workerId: string;
74	  sessionId: string | null | undefined;
75	  update: unknown;
76	}): Promise<GoalSessionUpdateResult> {
77	  if (!isAcpGoalNotification(args.update)) return { kind: "ignored", reason: "not_goal_update" };
78	  const runId = await resolveRunIdForGoalWorker(args.workerId);
79	  if (!runId) return { kind: "ignored", reason: "worker_not_persisted" };
80	  const { goalControl } = await import("@/server/runs/goal-control");
81	  const sessionId = typeof args.sessionId === "string" && args.sessionId.trim() ? args.sessionId.trim() : null;
82	  let current = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
83	  const metadata = goalMetadataEnvelope(args.update);
84	  const fallbackCommands = isGoalRecord(args.update)
85	    && args.update.sessionUpdate === "available_commands_update"
86	    && Array.isArray(args.update.availableCommands)

96	    }

98	    const operationToken = boundedGoalIdentityToken(`${sessionId}\u0000${providerGoalId}`);
99	    const created = await goalControl.putGoal({
100	      runId,
101	      goalId: `${runId}:${providerGoalId}`,
102	      expectedRevision: 0,

104	      principalId: `acp:${args.workerId}`,
105	      endpoint: "goal.put",
106	      objective: normalizedMetadata.value.objective,
107	    });
108	    if (!created.ok) return { kind: "rejected", reason: created.code };
109	    const attached = await goalControl.attachLease({

114	      acpSessionId: sessionId,

116	    if (!attached.ok) return { kind: "rejected", reason: attached.code };
117	    await goalControl.markControlApplied(attached.snapshot, "extension");
118	    current = attached.snapshot;
119	  }
120	  if (!current) return { kind: "ignored", reason: "goal_absent" };
121	  if (
122	    !sessionId
123	    || current.workerId !== args.workerId
124	    || current.acpSessionId !== sessionId
125	  ) {
126	    const { emitNamedEvent } = await import("@/server/events/named-events");
127	    emitNamedEvent({
128	      kind: "goal.stale_lease_ignored",
129	      runId,
130	      goalId: current.goalId,
131	      workerId: args.workerId,
132	      leaseGeneration: current.workerId === args.workerId ? current.leaseGeneration : 0,
133	      currentLeaseGeneration: current.leaseGeneration,
134	    });
135	    return { kind: "rejected", reason: "stale_lease" };
136	  }
137	
138	  let update: Parameters<typeof goalControl.applyProviderUpdate>[0];
139	  if (metadata) {
140	    const normalized = normalizedMetadata ?? normalizeAcpGoalMetadata(metadata);
141	    if (!normalized.ok) {
142	      await emitGoalPayloadRejection({ runId, goalId: current.goalId, workerId: args.workerId, reason: normalized.reason });
143	      return { kind: "rejected", reason: normalized.reason };
144	    }
145	    if (normalized.value.objective && normalized.value.objective !== current.objective) {

151	        workerId: args.workerId,
152	        reason: "provider_objective_conflict_durable_wins",

155	    update = {

157	      goalId: current.goalId,

162	      status: normalized.value.status ?? undefined,
163	      capabilities: normalized.value.capabilities,
164	      validationState: normalized.value.validationState,

166	  } else if (fallbackCommands) {

171	      workerId: args.workerId,
172	      acpSessionId: sessionId,

187	      goalId: current.goalId,

199	  if (!result.ok) {

225	export async function initializeWorkerGoalSession(
226	  workerId: string,
227	  sessionId: string,
228	  options: {
229	    dispatch?: (snapshot: import("@/shared/goal-plan").GoalSnapshot) => Promise<
230	      | { kind: "dispatched"; method: "extension" | "slash" }
231	      | { kind: "deferred"; reason: "no_active_lease" }
232	      | { kind: "unsupported"; reason: string }
233	    >;
234	  } = {},
235	) {
236	  const runId = await resolveRunIdForGoalWorker(workerId);
237	  if (!runId) return { kind: "ignored" as const, reason: "worker_not_persisted" as const };
238	  const [{ goalControl }, { emitNamedEvent }, { goalOutboxDispatcher }] = await Promise.all([
239	    import("@/server/runs/goal-control"),
240	    import("@/server/events/named-events"),
241	    import("@/server/runs/goal-outbox"),
242	  ]);
243	  const current = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
244	  if (!current || !current.visible || current.status === "cleared") {
245	    return { kind: "ignored" as const, reason: "goal_absent" as const };
246	  }
247	  const attached = await goalControl.attachLease({
248	    runId,
249	    goalId: current.goalId,
250	    expectedRevision: current.revision,
251	    workerId,
252	    acpSessionId: sessionId,
253	  });
254	  if (!attached.ok) {
255	    emitNamedEvent({
256	      kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
257	      runId,
258	      goalId: current.goalId,
259	      workerId,
260	      reason: attached.code,
261	    });
262	    return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason: attached.code };
263	  }
264	  const snapshot = attached.snapshot;
265	  if (!attached.replayed && current.workerId !== workerId) {
266	    emitNamedEvent({
267	      kind: "goal.worker_transferred",
268	      runId,
269	      goalId: snapshot.goalId,
270	      previousWorkerId: current.workerId,
271	      workerId,
272	      leaseGeneration: snapshot.leaseGeneration,
273	    });
274	  }
275	  let settledSnapshot = snapshot;
276	  try {
277	    const { createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator, goalControlDispatchCoordinator } = await import(
278	      "@/server/runs/goal-control-dispatch"
279	    );
280	    const coordinator = options.dispatch
281	      ? createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator({
282	          getGoal: (goalRunId) => goalControl.getGoal(goalRunId),
283	          isControlSettled: (goalSnapshot) => goalControl.isControlSettled(goalSnapshot),
284	          dispatch: (goalSnapshot) => options.dispatch!(goalSnapshot),
285	          markControlApplied: (goalSnapshot, method, action) => goalControl.markControlApplied(goalSnapshot, method, action),
286	        })
287	      : goalControlDispatchCoordinator;
288	    const dispatched = await coordinator.dispatch(snapshot, "set");
289	    if (dispatched.snapshot) settledSnapshot = dispatched.snapshot;
290	    if (dispatched.kind === "unsupported") {
291	        await goalControl.recordControlFailure(dispatched.snapshot, "unsupported", dispatched.reason);
292	        await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
293	        emitNamedEvent({
294	          kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
295	          runId,
296	          goalId: dispatched.snapshot.goalId,
297	          workerId,
298	          reason: dispatched.reason,
299	        });
300	        emitNamedEvent({
301	          kind: "error.surfaced",
302	          code: "goal.action.unsupported",
303	          message: "The active agent does not advertise goal control support.",
304	          surface: "banner",
305	          runId,
306	          workerId,
307	        });
308	        return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason: dispatched.reason };
309	    }
310	    if (dispatched.kind === "superseded" || dispatched.kind === "deferred") {
311	      emitNamedEvent({
312	        kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
313	        runId,
314	        goalId: snapshot.goalId,
315	        workerId,
316	        reason: dispatched.reason,
317	      });
318	      return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason: dispatched.reason };
319	    }
320	  } catch (error) {
321	      const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
322	      await goalControl.recordControlFailure(snapshot, "transport", reason);
323	      await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
324	      emitNamedEvent({ kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed", runId, goalId: snapshot.goalId, workerId, reason });
325	      emitNamedEvent({
326	        kind: "error.surfaced",
327	        code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
328	        message: reason,
329	        surface: "banner",
330	        runId,
331	        workerId,
332	      });
333	      return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason };
334	  }
335	  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
336	  emitNamedEvent({
337	    kind: "goal.reconciliation.completed",
338	    runId,
339	    goalId: settledSnapshot.goalId,
340	    workerId,
341	    revision: settledSnapshot.revision,
342	    leaseGeneration: settledSnapshot.leaseGeneration,
343	  });
344	  return { kind: "accepted" as const, snapshot: settledSnapshot };
345	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:45:56.825Z

This goal came from an ACP `_meta.goal` metadata envelope Codex sent at 17:36 UTC, with the objective normalized from the user's `/goal` command—capitalized "Fully" suggests Codex reformatted the raw text. It's Codex's goal extension, sent without an accompanying plan, though I'd expect a plan to follow if it supports that extension.

Stepping back, the real issue is the user's complaint that the plan panel is empty. The root cause seems to be that OmniHarness never derives plan items when the agent doesn't emit an ACP plan itself, even though the goal objective points to a specific plan markdown file on disk containing a checklist. I should check whether that file actually exists and has items I can parse.

# Caption Font Sizing Contract Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use ultrapowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or ultrapowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Keep both sizing behaviors (fixed shared size, and per-cue box filling) as explicit preset choices, give Karaoke a calibrated default instead of the 48 px fallback, make the terminal preset honor a large authored size, remove the editor's font-size ceiling, and fix the typewriter caret geometry and background parity.

**Architecture:** The layout engines already implement both behaviors. `growFontToFitBox` is per-cue font growth in the Karaoke and simple-row engines; `fillCaptionBox` / `fillCaptionBoxMode` is the Stacked engine's per-row geometry fill. Neither changes meaning. The terminal problem is engine ownership: the preset runs on cc-v4, which seeds word size from `targetBox.height × anchorScale` and never scales above 1, and which by contract discards a root `fontSize` as a layout input. The fix moves the terminal preset to the plain engine, where the authored size is the layout size and only overflow shrinks it. The remaining work is: an engine-aware default size table in the JS reference mirrored in Rust; an explicit `growFontToFitBox` on every stored preset via the shared write canonicalizer; a numeric size input beside the slider; a line caret by default, a narrow block caret, and DOM replacement that hides glyph nodes rather than word groups. Every change follows `docs/superpowers/caption-render-path-sync.md`: JS reference first, Rust mirror, WASM rebuild and sync, consumers, parity tests.

**Tech Stack:** JS reference under `src/algo`, Rust crates `cc_captions` and `cc_motion`, `wasm-layout` / `wasm-motion` bindings, `@cloudcaptions/shared` canonicalizers, Next.js editor and admin surfaces, Bun tests, Playwright browser tests.

**North Star Product:** A caption style declares its sizing intent once. Fixed-size presets read the same everywhere, box-filling presets fill every box, and a designer can type any authoring-pixel size on a size-owning engine and see exactly that size in the editor, the admin preview, and the export.

**Current Milestone:** The changes above, with deterministic tests, parity gates, regenerated seed previews, and a real-browser check of Karaoke stability, terminal at 130 px and 300 px, and the caret.

**Final Functionality Standard:** Every task lands real product behavior in the shared engines, real persistence, and real UI. No feature flags, no preview-only paths, no CSS scaling. Completion means `verify:caption-contract`, `verify:motion-contract`, the Rust suites, the full unit sweep, `qa:smoke`, and the browser checks in Task 10 all pass.

---

## Scope notes

- Isolation: work on the checked-out branch in this repository. No worktree, no branch (repo policy).
- The report's "grow the whole set together" mode is **not** in scope. The user confirmed the two wanted behaviors are fixed size and per-cue fill, and both exist.
- First-box initialization is **not** in scope. A calibrated preset default covers every case it would have served.
- cc-v4 sizing is **not** changed. Its root `fontSize` is deliberately not a layout input (`packages/cloudcaptions-shared/src/importance-style-owners.ts:161-168`, plan `docs/plans/2026-08-06-importance-tier-font-ownership.md`). On cc-v4 the Size control affects paint fallback only. The terminal preset therefore changes engine rather than cc-v4 changing contract.
- The admin preview already lays out at the preset's saved bounds and projects with `frameStyleLoopPreviewForViewport`. Task 8 pins that with a test; it does not change it.
- `scripts/qa/release-gate-preflight.mjs:120-143` rejects any selected non-cc-v4 preset. Phosphor Terminal is not the release-gate `CAPTION_STYLE_ID`, so moving it to plain does not affect that gate. Do not select it there.
- The local `sqlite.db` has no row named Terminal. The code-defined seed is `preset_phosphor_terminal` in `src/server/db/global-style-presets.ts:271-313` (JetBrains Mono, cc-v4, `fontSize: 46`, explicit block caret). That seed is the terminal preset this plan changes.

## Findings that shape the plan

| Finding | Location |
| --- | --- |
| cc-v4 seeds size from box height × `anchorScale`, scale capped at 1 | `src/algo/cc-v4/index.js:135-147` |
| cc-v4 takes an explicit size only from tier/word owner styles, and the owner normalizer deletes `fontSize` | `src/algo/cc-v4/index.js:172-180`, `importance-style-owners.ts:161-168` |
| Plain keeps the authored size when it fits and shrinks on overflow | `src/algo/simple-row-engine.js:180-207`, `src/algo/plain-engine.js:15-40` |
| Caption font slider max is 160 | `src/app/app/editor/[editId]/caption-style-property-tabs.tsx:349-352` |
| A test forbids a `type="number"` font-size input | `tests/caption-style-panel.test.tsx:1750-1751` |
| Shared style-chip validator accepts 8..600 | `packages/cloudcaptions-shared/src/default-style-chip.ts:41-53,109-113` |
| Karaoke fallback 48 (JS / Rust) | `src/algo/karaoke-layout-engine.js:79`, `rust/crates/cc_captions/src/karaoke.rs:59` |
| Stacked default 80 (JS / app) | `src/algo/stacked-engine.js:68-73,234-240`, `src/lib/wasm-layout/stacked-composition-executor.ts:36-47` |

1	export interface ParsedPlanItem {
2	  id: string;
3	  phase: string | null;
4	  title: string;
5	  sourceLine: number;
6	  details?: string;
7	}

9	export interface ParsedPlan {
10	  markdown: string;
11	  items: ParsedPlanItem[];
12	}

14	export function parsePlan(markdown: string): ParsedPlan {
15	  const lines = markdown.split("\n");
16	  const items: ParsedPlanItem[] = [];
17	  let currentPhase: string | null = null;
18	
19	  for (const [index, line] of lines.entries()) {
20	    const phaseMatch = line.match(/^##\s+(.+)$/);
21	    if (phaseMatch) {
22	      currentPhase = phaseMatch[1].trim();
23	      continue;
24	    }
25	
26	    const itemMatch = line.match(/^(?:- \[ \]|\d+\.)\s+(.+)$/);
27	    if (itemMatch) {
28	      items.push({
29	        id: `item-${index + 1}`,
30	        phase: currentPhase,
31	        title: itemMatch[1].trim(),
32	        sourceLine: index + 1,
33	        details: collectItemDetails(lines, index),
34	      });
35	    }
36	  }
37	
38	  return { markdown, items };
39	}
40	
41	function collectItemDetails(lines: string[], itemIndex: number) {
42	  const details: string[] = [];
43	
44	  for (let index = itemIndex + 1; index < lines.length; index += 1) {
45	    const line = lines[index];
46	    if (/^#{1,6}\s+(.+)$/.test(line) || /^(?:- \[ \]|\d+\.)\s+(.+)$/.test(line)) {
47	      break;
48	    }
49	    if (line.trim().length > 0) {
50	      details.push(line.trim());
51	    }
52	  }
53	
54	  return details.join("\n");
55	}

Since the plan file already has checkbox items, the fix should resolve references to an existing markdown plan file from the goal or initial message, then hydrate the plan with parsed checklist items and set planSource accordingly, so the panel shows real items instead of "No plan items yet." I need to think carefully about the minimal, architecture-consistent hook point—likely when a goal is created or updated.

I'm considering reusing the existing plan parser to convert file contents into structured plan items, and setting planSource to a "uri" kind so the UI both shows progress and offers an "open artifact" link, which seems like the ideal outcome.

But I'm second-guessing whether a server-derived plan conflicts with provider authority—since provider updates flow through their own path, I need to check how goal control handles lease and revision logic before wiring this in, to avoid breaking that mechanism. Actually, I should pause and reconsider whether this is really the right fix the user wants before diving into code.

This is clearly a bug report from the user, so I should just fix it rather than just explaining. Before committing to which "plan" surface to change, though, I want to verify there isn't another more obvious plan UI—like a planning artifacts panel or a dedicated plan view in the worker terminal—so I'm going to check that route and its rendering first.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:46:30.967Z

The ACP plan widget only appears when the agent emits a plan, so that's likely not it — it just wouldn't render. The "empty plan" symptom points more to the goal card's PlanProgress being rendered but with no data. I confirm the goal card is visible and pending for this run, so my plan is to implement server-side hydration of the goal plan from a referenced plan file.

Now I want to dig into goal-control.ts to see how it's currently handling this.

99	          }

101	          if (!run.rows[0]) return goalFailure("not_found", "The run does not exist.", null);
102	          await transaction.execute({
103	            sql: `INSERT INTO run_goals (
104	              run_id, goal_id, objective, status, revision, lease_generation,
105	              plan_json, plan_source_json, capabilities_json, transition_source,
106	              visible, started_at, updated_at
107	            ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'pending', 1, 0, '[]', '{"kind":"none"}', '{}', 'api', 1, ?, ?)`,
108	            args: [input.runId, input.goalId, validated.objective, now, now],
109	          });
110	          const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
111	          if (!snapshot) throw new Error("Goal insert did not produce a snapshot");
112	          await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, "goal.set.completed", now, this.randomId);
113	          return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
114	        }
115	        if (current.status === "cleared") {
116	          if (current.revision !== input.expectedRevision) {
117	            return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal revision is stale.", current);
118	          }
119	          if (current.goalId === input.goalId) {
120	            return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A cleared goal id cannot be revived.", current);
121	          }
122	          const nextRevision = current.revision + 1;
123	          await transaction.execute({
124	            sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET
125	                    goal_id = ?, objective = ?, status = 'pending', revision = ?,
126	                    plan_json = '[]', plan_source_json = '{"kind":"none"}',
127	                    validation_state_json = NULL, last_error = NULL, control_method = NULL,
128	                    transition_source = 'api', visible = 1, started_at = ?,
129	                    paused_at = NULL, resumed_at = NULL, completed_at = NULL, cleared_at = NULL,
130	                    updated_at = ?
131	                  WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status = 'cleared'`,
132	            args: [
133	              input.goalId,
134	              validated.objective,
135	              nextRevision,
136	              now,
137	              now,
138	              input.runId,
139	              current.goalId,
140	              input.expectedRevision,
141	            ],
142	          });
143	          const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
144	          if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== nextRevision || snapshot.goalId !== input.goalId) {
145	            return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The cleared goal changed before replacement was committed.", snapshot);
146	          }
147	          await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, "goal.set.completed", now, this.randomId);
148	          return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
149	        }

151	        if (conflict) return conflict;
152	        if (current.objective === validated.objective) {
153	          return { ok: true, snapshot: current, replayed: true };
154	        }
155	        const nextRevision = current.revision + 1;
156	        await transaction.execute({

158	                SET objective = ?, revision = ?, control_method = NULL, transition_source = 'api', updated_at = ?
159	                WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
160	          args: [validated.objective, nextRevision, now, input.runId, input.goalId, input.expectedRevision],
161	        });
162	        const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
163	        if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== nextRevision) {
164	          return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal changed before the edit was committed.", snapshot);
165	        }
166	        await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, "goal.updated", now, this.randomId);
167	        return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
168	      },

172	  async actionGoal(input: GoalActionRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
173	    return runGoalOperation(this.client, this.now, input, input.action, { action: input.action }, async (transaction, current, now) => {
174	      if (!current) return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
175	      const conflict = this.validateMutationFence(current, input);
176	      if (conflict) return conflict;
177	      if (current.status === "cleared") return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "The goal is already cleared.", current);
178	
179	      let nextStatus: GoalStatus;
180	      let eventKind: string;
181	      if (input.action === "pause") {
182	        if (!current.capabilities.pause) return goalFailure("unsupported_action", "The active agent cannot pause goals.", current);
183	        nextStatus = "paused";
184	        eventKind = "goal.paused";
185	      } else if (input.action === "resume" || input.action === "retry") {
186	        const supported = input.action === "retry" || current.capabilities.resume;
187	        if (!supported) return goalFailure("unsupported_action", "The active agent cannot resume goals.", current);
188	        nextStatus = "pursuing";
189	        eventKind = input.action === "retry" ? "goal.reconciliation.started" : "goal.resumed";
190	      } else {
191	        nextStatus = "cleared";
192	        eventKind = "goal.cleared";
193	      }
194	      if (!canTransitionGoalStatus(current.status, nextStatus)) {
195	        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", `Cannot ${input.action} a goal in ${current.status}.`, current);
196	      }
197	
198	      const revision = current.revision + 1;
199	      await transaction.execute({
200	        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET
201	                status = ?, revision = ?, transition_source = ?, visible = ?,
202	                control_method = NULL,
203	                paused_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'paused' THEN ? ELSE paused_at END,
204	                resumed_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'pursuing' THEN ? ELSE resumed_at END,
205	                cleared_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'cleared' THEN ? ELSE cleared_at END,
206	                updated_at = ?
207	              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
208	        args: [
209	          nextStatus, revision, input.action === "retry" ? "recovery" : "api",
210	          nextStatus === "cleared" ? 0 : 1,
211	          nextStatus, now, nextStatus, now, nextStatus, now, now,
212	          input.runId, input.goalId, input.expectedRevision,
213	        ],
214	      });
215	      const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
216	      if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== revision) {
217	        return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal changed before the action was committed.", snapshot);
218	      }
219	      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, eventKind, now, this.randomId);
220	      return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
221	    });
222	  }

228	  private async attachLeaseOnce(input: AttachGoalLeaseRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
229	    const transaction = await this.client.transaction("write");
230	    try {
231	      const current = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
232	      if (!current) {
233	        await transaction.commit();
234	        return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
235	      }
236	      const conflict = this.validateMutationFence(current, input);
237	      if (conflict) {
238	        await transaction.commit();
239	        return conflict;
240	      }
241	      if (current.status === "cleared") {
242	        await transaction.commit();
243	        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A cleared goal cannot acquire a lease.", current);
244	      }
245	      if (current.workerId === input.workerId && current.acpSessionId === input.acpSessionId) {
246	        await transaction.commit();
247	        return { ok: true, snapshot: current, replayed: true };
248	      }
249	      const now = this.now().getTime();
250	      const revision = current.revision + 1;
251	      const leaseGeneration = current.leaseGeneration + 1;
252	      await transaction.execute({
253	        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET worker_id = ?, acp_session_id = ?, lease_generation = ?, revision = ?,
254	                control_method = NULL, transition_source = 'reconciliation', updated_at = ?
255	              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
256	        args: [input.workerId, input.acpSessionId, leaseGeneration, revision, now, input.runId, input.goalId, input.expectedRevision],
257	      });
258	      const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
259	      if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== revision) {
260	        await transaction.rollback();
261	        return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal changed before the lease was attached.", snapshot);
262	      }
263	      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, "goal.reconciled", now, this.randomId);
264	      await transaction.commit();
265	      return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
266	    } catch (error) {
267	      await transaction.rollback();
268	      throw error;
269	    }
270	  }

276	  private async applyProviderUpdateOnce(input: ProviderGoalUpdateRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
277	    const transaction = await this.client.transaction("write");
278	    try {
279	      const current = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
280	      if (!current) {
281	        await transaction.commit();
282	        return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
283	      }
284	      if (current.status === "cleared") {
285	        await transaction.commit();
286	        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A cleared goal cannot be updated.", current);
287	      }
288	      if (
289	        current.goalId !== input.goalId
290	        || current.revision !== input.expectedRevision
291	        || current.workerId !== input.workerId
292	        || current.acpSessionId !== input.acpSessionId
293	        || current.leaseGeneration !== input.leaseGeneration
294	      ) {
295	        await transaction.commit();
296	        return goalFailure("stale_lease", "The provider update belongs to a stale goal lease.", current);
297	      }
298	      const nextStatus = input.status ?? current.status;
299	      const validationState = input.validationState === undefined ? current.validationState : input.validationState;
300	      if (!canTransitionGoalStatus(current.status, nextStatus)) {
301	        await transaction.commit();
302	        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", `Cannot move a goal from ${current.status} to ${nextStatus}.`, current);
303	      }
304	      if (nextStatus === "completed" && validationState?.status !== "passed") {
305	        await transaction.commit();
306	        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "A goal must pass validation before completion.", current);
307	      }
308	      const plan = input.plan ?? current.plan;
309	      const planSource = input.planSource ?? current.planSource;
310	      const capabilities = input.capabilities ?? current.capabilities;
311	      const lastError = input.lastError === undefined
312	        ? validationState?.status === "failed"
313	          ? validationState.message ?? "Goal validation failed."
314	          : input.validationState !== undefined && (validationState?.status === "validating" || validationState?.status === "passed")
315	            ? null
316	            : current.lastError
317	        : input.lastError;
318	      const unchanged = nextStatus === current.status
319	        && JSON.stringify(plan) === JSON.stringify(current.plan)
320	        && JSON.stringify(planSource) === JSON.stringify(current.planSource)
321	        && JSON.stringify(capabilities) === JSON.stringify(current.capabilities)
322	        && JSON.stringify(validationState) === JSON.stringify(current.validationState)
323	        && lastError === current.lastError;
324	      if (unchanged) {
325	        await transaction.commit();
326	        return { ok: true, snapshot: current, replayed: true };
327	      }
328	      const now = this.now().getTime();
329	      const revision = current.revision + 1;
330	      await transaction.execute({
331	        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET status = ?, revision = ?, plan_json = ?, plan_source_json = ?,
332	                capabilities_json = ?, validation_state_json = ?, last_error = ?, transition_source = 'acp',
333	                completed_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'completed' THEN ? ELSE completed_at END,
334	                updated_at = ?
335	              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
336	                AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
337	        args: [
338	          nextStatus, revision, JSON.stringify(plan), JSON.stringify(planSource), JSON.stringify(capabilities),
339	          validationState === null ? null : JSON.stringify(validationState), lastError,
340	          nextStatus, now, now, input.runId, input.goalId, input.expectedRevision, input.leaseGeneration,
341	          input.workerId, input.acpSessionId,
342	        ],
343	      });
344	      const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
345	      if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== revision) {
346	        await transaction.rollback();
347	        return goalFailure("stale_lease", "The provider update lost its lease fence.", snapshot);
348	      }
349	      const validationChanged = JSON.stringify(validationState) !== JSON.stringify(current.validationState);
350	      const eventKind = input.planSource?.kind === "none" ? "goal.plan.removed"
351	        : input.plan || input.planSource ? "goal.plan.updated"
352	          : nextStatus === "completed" ? "goal.completed"
353	            : validationChanged && validationState?.status === "validating" ? "goal.validation.started"
354	              : validationChanged && validationState?.status === "passed" ? "goal.validation.completed"
355	                : validationChanged && validationState?.status === "failed" ? "goal.validation.failed"
356	                  : "goal.updated";
357	      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, eventKind, now, this.randomId);
358	      await transaction.commit();
359	      return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
360	    } catch (error) {
361	      await transaction.rollback();
362	      throw error;
363	    }
364	  }

369	            WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
370	              AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? LIMIT 1`,

383	  async markControlApplied(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, method: GoalControlMethod, action: GoalMutationAction = "set") {
384	    const marker = `${method}:${snapshot.acpSessionId}:${snapshot.leaseGeneration}:${snapshot.revision}`;
385	    const result = await this.client.execute({

387	            WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND lease_generation = ?
388	              AND worker_id = ? AND acp_session_id = ? AND control_method IS NULL
389	              AND (status != 'cleared' OR ? = 'clear')`,
390	      args: [

392	        this.now().getTime(),
393	        snapshot.runId,
394	        snapshot.goalId,
395	        snapshot.revision,
396	        snapshot.leaseGeneration,
397	        snapshot.workerId,
398	        snapshot.acpSessionId,
399	        action,
400	      ],
401	    });
402	    return result.rowsAffected === 1;
403	  }

414	  private async recordControlFailureOnce(
415	    snapshot: GoalSnapshot,
416	    kind: "unsupported" | "transport",
417	    message: string,
418	  ): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {

441	      if (!canTransitionGoalStatus(current.status, nextStatus)) {

464	        ],
465	      });
466	      const next = await selectGoal(transaction, snapshot.runId);
467	      if (!next || next.revision !== revision) {
468	        await transaction.rollback();
469	        return goalFailure("stale_lease", "The goal changed before the control failure was recorded.", next);
470	      }
471	      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, next, kind === "unsupported" ? "goal.limited" : "goal.updated", now, this.randomId);
472	      await transaction.commit();
473	      return { ok: true, snapshot: next, replayed: false };
474	    } catch (error) {
475	      await transaction.rollback();
476	      throw error;
477	    }
478	  }

480	  async finalizeOperation(runId: string, operationId: string, result: GoalMutationResult) {
481	    return finalizeGoalOperation(this.client, this.now, runId, operationId, result);
482	  }
483	
484	  private validateMutationFence(current: GoalSnapshot, input: Pick<OperationIdentity, "goalId" | "expectedRevision">) {
485	    if (current.goalId !== input.goalId || current.revision !== input.expectedRevision) {
486	      return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal revision is stale.", current);
487	    }
488	    return null;
489	  }
490	}

export const GOAL_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1 as const;
export const GOAL_OBJECTIVE_MAX_LENGTH = 4_000;
export const GOAL_PLAN_MAX_ITEMS = 200;
export const GOAL_PLAN_ITEM_MAX_LENGTH = 2_000;
export const GOAL_PLAN_MARKDOWN_MAX_LENGTH = 100_000;
export const GOAL_PLAN_URI_MAX_LENGTH = 2_048;

export const GOAL_STATUSES = [
  "absent",
  "pending",
  "pursuing",
  "paused",
  "waiting_user",
  "blocked",
  "limited",
  "validating",
  "completed",
  "cleared",
  "error",
] as const;

export type GoalStatus = (typeof GOAL_STATUSES)[number];

export const GOAL_ACTIONS = ["pause", "resume", "clear", "retry"] as const;
export type GoalAction = (typeof GOAL_ACTIONS)[number];
export type GoalMutationAction = "set" | "edit" | GoalAction;
export type GoalMutationSource = "api" | "acp" | "reconciliation" | "recovery";

export const GOAL_PUBLISHED_EVENT_KINDS = [
  "goal.set.completed",
  "goal.updated",
  "goal.paused",
  "goal.resumed",
  "goal.cleared",
  "goal.plan.updated",
  "goal.plan.removed",
  "goal.validation.started",
  "goal.validation.completed",
  "goal.validation.failed",
  "goal.blocked",
  "goal.limited",
  "goal.completed",
  "goal.reconciled",
  "goal.reconciliation.started",
] as const;
export type GoalPublishedEventKind = (typeof GOAL_PUBLISHED_EVENT_KINDS)[number];

export const GOAL_PLAN_ITEM_STATUSES = [
  "pending",
  "in_progress",
  "blocked",
  "completed",
  "failed",
] as const;
export type GoalPlanItemStatus = (typeof GOAL_PLAN_ITEM_STATUSES)[number];

export interface GoalPlanItem {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  phase: string | null;
  status: GoalPlanItemStatus;
  order: number;
  providerId: string | null;
}

export type GoalPlanSource =
  | { kind: "none" }
  | { kind: "items" }
  | { kind: "markdown"; markdown: string }
  | { kind: "uri"; uri: string };

export interface GoalCapabilities {
  set: boolean;
  edit: boolean;
  pause: boolean;
  resume: boolean;
  clear: boolean;
  fallbackMethod: string | null;
}

export interface GoalValidationState {
  status: "idle" | "validating" | "passed" | "failed";
  message: string | null;
  updatedAt: string;
}

export interface GoalSnapshotProvenance {
  source: "server" | "cache";
  complete: boolean;
  eventCursor: number | null;
}

export interface GoalSnapshot {
  schemaVersion: typeof GOAL_SCHEMA_VERSION;
  runId: string;
  goalId: string;
  revision: number;
  leaseGeneration: number;
  objective: string;
  status: GoalStatus;
  startedAt: string;
  pausedAt: string | null;
  resumedAt: string | null;
  completedAt: string | null;
  clearedAt: string | null;
  updatedAt: string;
  workerId: string | null;
  acpSessionId: string | null;
  plan: GoalPlanItem[];
  planSource: GoalPlanSource;
  capabilities: GoalCapabilities;
  lastError: string | null;
  validationState: GoalValidationState | null;
  visible: boolean;
  provenance: GoalSnapshotProvenance;
}

export interface GoalMutationInput {
  goalId: string;
  expectedRevision: number;
  operationId: string;
}

export interface PutGoalInput extends GoalMutationInput {
  objective: string;
}

export interface GoalActionInput extends GoalMutationInput {
  action: GoalAction;
}

export type GoalMutationResult =
  | { ok: true; snapshot: GoalSnapshot; replayed: boolean }
  | {
      ok: false;
      code: "not_found" | "revision_conflict" | "invalid_transition" | "unsupported_action" | "operation_conflict" | "stale_lease" | "invalid_objective" | "provider_error" | "persistence_error";
      message: string;
      snapshot: GoalSnapshot | null;
    };

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:47:17.061Z

I'm working out a fix where, if an agent creates a goal without a plan, the system derives one from a markdown plan file referenced in the objective, then displays its checklist items on the goal card. I'm sketching a new hydration module that extracts the plan file path from the objective text and project context.

I'm also considering extending the plan parser to recognize checked boxes as completed items, not just pending ones, so the panel reflects real progress instead of showing an empty plan when work has already happened.

On the second issue, I'm figuring out where to wire the hydration in — since `putGoal` is a pure transactional DB call, it seems better to hydrate the plan outside that path and feed it through an update method rather than duplicating parsing logic inside the goal control layer.

Applying a server-derived plan through `applyProviderUpdate` would fit within existing lease fencing since goals already attach a lease at creation, but tagging the transition source as 'acp' would be misleading — maybe a 'reconciliation' source is more honest. A dedicated hydration method on the goal control service is another option, though it adds more surface area, so I want to trace back to where goals actually get created to find the cleanest insertion point.

Both creation paths — the ACP session handler and the HTTP PUT route — end up with a snapshot, so a shared hydration helper invoked after creation or objective changes would cover both cases. It should skip hydration if the provider already supplied a real plan, and if a concurrent provider update causes a stale-lease conflict during hydration, it's fine to just skip since the provider's real plan will win anyway.

Reconsidering though: reusing `applyProviderUpdate` for a server-derived plan would mislabel it with `transition_source='acp'`, which misleads debugging given the project's strong observability conventions requiring typed named events for server-side decisions. So I think the right move is a dedicated path — adding an `applyDerivedPlan` method to GoalControlService that takes the run/goal IDs, expected revision, plan, and plan source, and returns a proper mutation result.

This would update plan_json/plan_source_json with transition_source='reconciliation', gate on runId+goalId+revision, and enqueue a `goal.plan.updated` event, with no lease requirement since it's server-derived. It's a small, clean addition mirroring existing patterns, though I need to watch for revision bumps conflicting with in-flight provider updates that hold an expectedRevision — that's just the normal contention model already used elsewhere. I also need to add new named event kinds like `goal.plan.derived` and possibly `goal.plan.derivation_skipped`, since silent early returns are treated as bugs in this codebase.

Checking whether plan_items feeds any UI, I found it's only referenced in state managers/home-types, never in a .tsx file — so it's dead weight and the goal plan is the real surface to focus on. Looking at GoalPlanCard, when planSource.kind is "uri" it shows an "open artifact" button plus PlanProgress, so a derived plan could set planSource to a file URI letting the user open the plan file while items populate progress — I need to verify onOpenPlanArtifact is properly wired.

A simpler, safer alternative might be setting planSource to `{kind: "items"}` so PlanProgress just shows items without the artifact button, though that loses the link to the file. I also want to confirm the goal card only expands when the user manually expands it, and think about whether progress should update live as the agent checks items off in the file, versus a one-shot hydration at goal creation that would go stale.

Re-reading the file on every snapshot seems too expensive. A better cadence might be to re-derive the plan at turn boundaries — when the worker goes idle — plus at goal creation, so I should look for where turn completion is handled in the agent runtime manager.

    try {
      const contentBlocks: Array<acp.ContentBlock> = [{ type: "text", text: prompt }];
      if (imageAttachments?.length) {
        for (const img of imageAttachments) {
          try {
            const buffer = await readFile(img.path);
            const data = buffer.toString("base64");
            contentBlocks.push({
              type: "image",
              data,

          } catch (error) {
            // Skip an unreadable image rather than failing the whole prompt,
            // but never silently: the agent is about to answer a question

      const responseRecord = asRecord(response);

      record.lastText = record.currentText;

      record.updatedAt = nowIso();
      return {

        response: record.lastText,
      };
    } catch (error) {
      record.state = error instanceof RuntimeHttpError && error.statusCode === 409 ? "stopped" : "error";

  async cancelAgentTurn(name: string) {
    const record = this.agents.get(name);

    assertAgentCanReceiveRequest(record);
    const cancelParams = { sessionId: record.sessionId } as Parameters<acp.ClientSideConnection["cancel"]>[0];

    const cancelledElicitations = this.cancelAllPendingElicitations(record);
    record.updatedAt = nowIso();
    if (record.state === "working") {
      record.state = "idle";
    }
    return { ok: true, name: record.name, cancelledPermissions, cancelledElicitations };
  }

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:47:59.005Z

I realize `worker_turn_completed` isn't fired in direct mode, so I should check `sync.ts` for the direct-control turn completion path. Maybe a simpler fix is to lazily refresh the derived plan on read with an mtime-based cache, since `goalControl.getGoal` gets called by the events snapshot.

Actually the cleaner approach is persisting the refresh so events fire correctly for the frontend rather than relying on read-time derivation, which wouldn't update live via SSE. I'll design a `refreshDerivedGoalPlan(runId)` function that loads the goal, skips unless the plan is server-derived or empty, resolves the plan file from the objective, and checks its mtime to decide whether to re-parse.

I still need to figure out where to call this refresh — likely at goal creation, after HTTP goal writes, and at worker turn end — and whether to track "last derived mtime" via an in-memory map or just rely on comparing derived JSON to skip redundant writes, which seems simpler. The trickier issue is distinguishing a server-derived plan from a provider-supplied one so refresh never overwrites a real provider plan, which probably means introducing a distinct plan source kind rather than reusing the existing "uri" kind.

I realize file-type provider plans always come with empty items, so a "uri" planSource with non-empty items must be the derived one, making the refresh rule "kind is uri, matches derivedUri, and plan.length > 0" safe enough despite edge cases. Still, I'm weighing whether it's more honest to just add a distinct source kind instead of overloading "uri", though that touches more shared code.

function requiredString(value: unknown, field: string) {
  if (typeof value !== "string" || !value) throw new TypeError(`${field} must be a non-empty string`);
  return value;
}

function nullableString(value: unknown, field: string) {
  if (value === null) return null;
  if (typeof value !== "string") throw new TypeError(`${field} must be a string or null`);
  return value;
}

function nonNegativeInteger(value: unknown, field: string) {
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(value) || Number(value) < 0) throw new TypeError(`${field} must be a non-negative integer`);
  return Number(value);
}

function parsePlanSource(value: unknown): GoalPlanSource {
  if (!isRecord(value) || !["none", "items", "markdown", "uri"].includes(String(value.kind))) {
    throw new TypeError("planSource is invalid");
  }
  if (value.kind === "markdown") {
    if (typeof value.markdown !== "string" || value.markdown.length > GOAL_PLAN_MARKDOWN_MAX_LENGTH) {
      throw new TypeError("planSource markdown is invalid");
    }
    return { kind: "markdown", markdown: value.markdown };
  }
  if (value.kind === "uri") {
    if (typeof value.uri !== "string" || value.uri.length > GOAL_PLAN_URI_MAX_LENGTH) {
      throw new TypeError("planSource uri is invalid");
    }
    return { kind: "uri", uri: value.uri };
  }
  return { kind: value.kind as "none" | "items" };
}

function parsePlanItem(value: unknown): GoalPlanItem {
  if (!isRecord(value)) throw new TypeError("plan item must be an object");
  const status = String(value.status);
  if (!GOAL_PLAN_ITEM_STATUSES.includes(status as GoalPlanItemStatus)) throw new TypeError("plan item status is invalid");
  const title = requiredString(value.title, "plan item title");
  if (title.length > GOAL_PLAN_ITEM_MAX_LENGTH) throw new TypeError("plan item title is too long");
  return {
    id: requiredString(value.id, "plan item id"),

    phase: nullableString(value.phase, "plan item phase"),

I should guard against clobbering the provider using the established rule, though I want to double-check one more thing before finalizing.

Actually, does deriving the plan from the file match what the user actually wants, or should it reflect an ACP plan from the agent? The agent didn't make a plan — the plan the user cares about is the markdown file they wrote for implementation, so deriving from it is correct and gives live progress through checkboxes. Now I want to verify whether those checkboxes were actually ticked off by the agent.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-05T19:48:27.183Z

All 56 plan items are unchecked, so deriving the list at least turns "no plan items" into a real 0/56 checklist rather than an empty state — that's the actual fix, even though the agent never ticked boxes despite implementing the feature. This might look odd to the user since the work was done but the plan file wasn't updated to reflect it, but showing 0/56 honestly reflects that the checkboxes were never marked.

I'll keep the run-level plans/plan_items table untouched since nothing reads it, and I won't repoint the run's plan path since that's riskier than needed for this fix. My plan is: update the parser to recognize checked boxes and expose completion status, add a new module to resolve and derive plan items per run, extend goal-control with an apply function, add new event kinds, and hook this into goal creation/attachment, the goals HTTP route, and turn-end — then write tests and verify readiness.ts isn't broken by the parser change.
